All facts confirmed. The draft has a stray opening line ("I have enough verified detail. Writing the section now.") that must be cut. Material 3 tier names confirmed (reference/system/component). DTCG draft is at 2025.10 — I'll update "still a draft (2025)" to be precise. Here's the complete revised section.

# 26. App design systems, tokens & cross-platform

The meta-layer: shipping one coherent product across iOS, Android, desktop, web, and wearables without flattening each platform into a lowest-common-denominator clone. Governing principle: **tokenize intent, not pixels** — share the semantic decision (this surface is "primary," this elevation is "raised"), let each platform resolve it to its own native value. The recurring failure mode this section exists to prevent: web-component thinking forced onto native, where a single hardcoded design ships to all platforms and feels wrong on every one.

## 26.1 Design tokens for apps

Tokens are named, platform-agnostic design decisions stored as data, transformed into platform code. The three-tier model (Material calls them reference/system/component; Salesforce and the broader industry say primitive/semantic/component) is the cross-vendor consensus.

- **Primitive / reference / global token** — the raw literal value: `blue-500 = #1A73E8`, `space-4 = 16dp`, `font-size-3 = 17pt`. Context-free, never consumed directly by a component. Trap: a component referencing a primitive (`button.bg = blue-500`) breaks theming — the whole point of the next tier is to forbid this.
- **Semantic / system / alias token** — names the *intent*, points at a primitive: `color.primary → blue-500`, `surface.container → grey-100`. This is the tier where light/dark/high-contrast/dynamic theming lives — swap what `color.primary` resolves to, every consumer follows. Reach for semantic tokens for anything a designer reasons about by meaning. Trap: under-naming — a flat list of `color.brand1…brand9` with no role semantics is just primitives with extra steps.
- **Component token** — scopes a semantic token to one component's slot: `button.filled.container.color → color.primary`, `fab.container.elevation → elevation.level3`. Lets you retheme one component without touching the global system. Material 3 ships these as a first-class tier; many teams skip it until a component needs to diverge from system defaults. Trap: minting a component token for every property explodes the count into the thousands and nobody can audit it — add the tier only where a real override exists.
- **Token type taxonomy** — color, typography (family/weight/size/line-height/tracking), spacing, sizing, elevation/shadow, shape/corner-radius, motion (duration + easing), opacity, border, z-index/layering, and increasingly *state* tokens (hover/pressed/focus/disabled layer opacities — Material's state-layer model). Each platform consumes a subset: elevation is a Material primitive but on iOS is expressed as layering + material blur, not a numeric `dp` shadow.
- **Style Dictionary (Amazon)** — the de-facto build tool: author tokens once in JSON/JSON5, transform to per-platform output — CSS custom properties, iOS Swift/`UIColor` + Asset Catalog, Android XML resources + Compose, Flutter Dart, even Figma variables. Defines "transforms" (unit/color-format conversion: `16` → `16dp` / `16pt` / `1rem`) and "formats" (file templates). v4 supports both legacy and DTCG (`$value`) token formats — but not mixed in one instance. Trap: a transform that emits `dp` for iOS or `pt` for Android — get the platform's native unit right or layout drifts.
- **W3C Design Tokens Format Module (DTCG)** — the emerging interchange standard (`.tokens.json`, `$value`/`$type`/`$description`). Still a working draft (latest editor's draft `2025.10`, explicitly "not to be implemented directly") yet already wired into Figma variable export, Tokens Studio, and Style Dictionary v4+. Date-sensitive: load-bearing for tool interop, but the spec is not frozen — pin your tooling versions. (→ §27 emerging)
- **Token naming convention** — `category.type.item.subitem.state`, e.g. `color.text.primary.disabled`. Consistency here is what makes Figma↔code sync mechanical. Trap: encoding the *value* in the name (`color.blue.primary`) — when brand goes purple, the name lies.

## 26.2 Platform UI frameworks & how each expresses tokens

The native and near-native declarative frameworks model components and theming differently; tokens land in each via different idioms.

| | SwiftUI | Jetpack Compose | Flutter |
|---|---|---|---|
| Owner | Apple | Google | Google |
| Renders | UIKit/AppKit-native (real platform controls) | Material-native (Android views) | own Skia/Impeller engine (pixel-drawn, not native widgets) |
| Theme primitive | `Environment` values, `Color`/`Font` assets, `@Environment(\.colorScheme)` | `MaterialTheme { colorScheme, typography, shapes }` via `CompositionLocal` | `ThemeData` / `Theme.of(context)`; `ColorScheme`, `TextTheme` |
| Token injection | Asset Catalog color sets (Any/Dark/high-contrast variants) + Swift constants | `ColorScheme` built from token values; dynamic color from wallpaper | `ColorScheme.fromSeed()` or explicit token map into `ThemeData` |
| Dark mode | automatic via dynamic colors in catalog | `darkColorScheme()` / `dynamicDarkColorScheme()` | `ThemeData.dark()` + `themeMode` |

- **SwiftUI theming** — there is no global "theme object"; semantic color lives in the **Asset Catalog** (named colors with Any/Dark/high-contrast appearances) and flows through `Color("primary")` + the `Environment`. iOS 26 / macOS Tahoe 26 add the `.glassEffect()` modifier and `GlassEffectContainer` for **Liquid Glass** (WWDC 2025) — controls float in a navigation layer over the content layer. Trap: hardcoding `Color(hex:)` instead of catalog colors kills automatic dark/contrast adaptation (→ §19 accessibility).
- **Jetpack Compose theming** — `MaterialTheme` exposes `colorScheme`, `typography`, `shapes` through `CompositionLocal`; Material 3's `dynamicLightColorScheme(context)` / `dynamicDarkColorScheme(context)` derive the palette from the user's wallpaper (**Material You**, Android 12 / API 31+). Components read `MaterialTheme.colorScheme.primary` — that *is* the semantic-token consumption point. Trap: passing literal `Color(0xFF…)` into a composable instead of `colorScheme.*` opts the component out of dynamic color and dark mode.
- **Flutter theming** — `ThemeData` is one big token bag; Material 3 (`useMaterial3: true`, the default since Flutter 3.16 / Nov 2023) + `ColorScheme.fromSeed(seedColor:)` generates a tonal palette meeting M3 contrast targets. Because Flutter *draws* widgets rather than instantiating native ones, the same `Material` widget looks identical on iOS and Android by default — which is exactly the divergence trap (§26.4). Cupertino widgets exist but are a partial, lagging port of UIKit.
- **React Native** — components map to real native views; theming is library-territory (no built-in token system) — typically React Context + a JS token object, or a lib (Tamagui, Restyle, NativeWind, Unistyles). Adaptive behavior is your job. Trap: assuming `<Button>` renders the platform-native button — it doesn't unless the library wraps the native control; with the New Architecture (Fabric) the rendering host changed but the token story didn't.
- **Kotlin Multiplatform / Compose Multiplatform** — Compose UI rendered on iOS/desktop/web via Skia, like Flutter — shares the Material divergence caveat; UIKit interop exists but you're not getting native controls for free. Date-sensitive (2026): iOS target is stable but young — treat as hybrid, not "native iOS."
- **.NET MAUI (Microsoft)** — successor to Xamarin.Forms; one XAML/C# UI mapping to native controls per platform (iOS, Android, Windows, macOS via Catalyst), styled through resource dictionaries + `AppThemeBinding` for light/dark. The Microsoft-stack analog to Flutter's single-codebase model, but renders native widgets. Trap: the abstraction leaks — per-platform handlers are routine, not exceptional.

## 26.3 Adaptive components

One component declaration, platform-correct rendering at runtime — the holy grail and the sharpest trap.

- **Flutter `.adaptive` constructors** — `Switch.adaptive`, `Slider.adaptive`, `CircularProgressIndicator.adaptive`, `Checkbox.adaptive`, `Radio.adaptive` render Cupertino on iOS/macOS and Material elsewhere; `showAdaptiveDialog` / `AlertDialog.adaptive` likewise. Reach for these to get free platform-correctness on primitives. Trap: coverage is partial — most widgets have no adaptive variant, so a screen is adaptive in spots and uniformly-Material everywhere else, which reads as *more* broken than committing to one style.
- **SwiftUI cross-Apple adaptivity** — the same `List`, `NavigationStack`, `TabView`, `Form` recompile to the idiomatic shape per Apple platform: a `TabView` is a bottom tab bar on iPhone, a sidebar or top tab bar on iPad/macOS, a page indicator in `.tabViewStyle(.page)` contexts. `NavigationSplitView` collapses to a stack on compact widths automatically. This is adaptivity *within* one vendor's family — Apple owns the divergence so you don't author it. Trap: fighting it with fixed frames defeats the adaptation (→ §19 — fixed frames also break Dynamic Type).
- **Material adaptive / canonical layouts** — Material 3's adaptive library (window size classes: compact <600dp / medium 600–839dp / expanded ≥840dp, plus large/extra-large tiers) swaps navigation bar ↔ navigation rail ↔ navigation drawer by width, and offers list-detail / supporting-pane / feed canonical layouts. Reach for window size classes, never raw pixel breakpoints. Trap: treating a foldable's unfolded state as just "wide phone" — hinge/posture (`WindowLayoutInfo` / Jetpack WindowManager) needs explicit handling.
- **The adaptive-component contract** — an adaptive component is a promise that *behavior and platform-feel* differ while *the data and intent* stay constant. Tokens make this tractable: the component reads semantic tokens; each platform's theme resolves them natively. Trap: "adaptive" creeping into meaning "looks the same everywhere" — that's *uniform*, the opposite goal.

## 26.4 Cross-platform divergence strategy

The strategic choice that governs everything above: what stays identical, what's allowed to diverge. Decide this *before* tokenizing.

- **Uniform-brand (single-soul)** — one look, deliberately identical across platforms (Spotify, Instagram, most Flutter/RN apps). Brand recognition and a single design+build effort win; the cost is non-native gestures, wrong-feeling controls, and App Store friction. Reach for it when the brand *is* the product and the audience spans platforms equally. Trap: shipping Android's back-affordance behavior to iOS (or vice versa) — even uniform apps must honor each OS's system gestures and navigation model (→ §19 — system gestures carry a11y contracts).
- **Platform-native (when-in-Rome)** — embrace each platform's HIG fully: SwiftUI/UIKit feel on Apple, Material on Android, Fluent on Windows. Apps feel "right," ride free OS updates (Liquid Glass, Material You) and accessibility wins. Cost: N design tracks, N codebases or heavy abstraction. Reach for it for utilities, system-adjacent apps, and anywhere platform trust matters (banking, health). Trap: divergence with no shared token spine → the "same" product looks like two different products to a cross-device user.
- **Hybrid (shared core, native chrome)** — the common real answer: identical information architecture, content, semantic tokens, and brand color/type; divergent navigation patterns, controls, gestures, and system materials. Keep identical: data model, copy, brand palette, iconography intent, spacing rhythm. Let diverge: tab bar vs nav rail vs sidebar, action sheet vs menu, switch vs checkbox styling, blur/elevation expression, haptics (→ §11), date/picker controls. Trap: drawing the line at the *visual* layer instead of the *interaction* layer — copying iOS's swipe-back to Android while keeping Material colors is the worst of both.

### What to keep identical vs let diverge

| Keep identical (cross-platform) | Let diverge (per-platform) |
|---|---|
| Information architecture, screen inventory | Top-level navigation pattern (tab bar / rail / drawer / sidebar) |
| Semantic color roles, brand palette | Material expression: blur, elevation, glass, shadow |
| Type scale ratios, content hierarchy | System font (SF Pro / Roboto / Segoe UI) |
| Microcopy, terminology, error messages | Control widgets (switch, picker, date control) |
| Iconography meaning | Icon style (SF Symbols vs Material Symbols) |
| Core flows & state machines | Gestures, back/dismiss affordance, haptics |
| Spacing rhythm / grid intent | Transient feedback (snackbar vs toast vs banner) |

## 26.5 Theming architecture

Themes are resolved entirely at the semantic tier — primitives never change, consumers never branch on theme.

- **Light / dark** — two resolutions of every semantic color token. Material supplies `lightColorScheme`/`darkColorScheme`; Apple supplies Any/Dark appearances in the Asset Catalog; web uses `prefers-color-scheme` + CSS custom properties. Trap: dark mode by naive inversion — elevation, shadow, and "surface" semantics change meaning (Material *lightens* surfaces with elevation in dark, doesn't just flip), and pure-black OLED ≠ Material's dark-grey baseline.
- **Dynamic color (Material You, Android 12+)** — palette generated at runtime from the user's wallpaper via the HCT color space; your tokens must be *role-based* (`primary`, `secondary`, `tertiary`, `surface`) for it to work. Apple's analog is narrower: tinting, accent colors, system "tinted" app icons (iOS 18+), and the content-derived tint of Liquid Glass (iOS 26). Trap: brand teams hate ceding palette control — dynamic color is opt-in; don't force it on a brand-critical surface, and verify generated pairs still pass contrast (→ §19).
- **High-contrast & increased-contrast** — a *first-class theme tier*, not an afterthought. Material 3 ships `highContrastLightColorScheme` / `highContrastDarkColorScheme`; iOS exposes "Increase Contrast" via the Asset Catalog's high-contrast appearance variant and `UIAccessibility.isDarkerSystemColorsEnabled`; web has `prefers-contrast` / `forced-colors` (Windows High Contrast / Forced Colors Mode). Accessibility veto: if your token system can't express a high-contrast theme, it fails WCAG 2.2 contrast (1.4.3 / 1.4.11) for users who need it (→ §19). Trap: testing only the default theme — contrast bugs hide in the variant you didn't open.
- **Theme as data, not code** — a theme is a *value map* (semantic token → primitive), swappable at runtime without recompiling components. SwiftUI `Environment`, Compose `CompositionLocal`, Flutter `Theme.of(context)`, CSS custom properties all implement this. Trap: `if isDark { … }` branches inside a component — that's theming logic leaking out of the token tier; one missed branch and the component is half-themed.
- **Per-platform semantic overrides** — the semantic layer can resolve differently per platform: `elevation.level2` → a `3dp` tonal+shadow surface on Android, a material-blur + hairline on iOS, an Acrylic/Mica layer on Windows. Same intent, native expression. This is how hybrid strategy (§26.4) is implemented inside the token system itself.

## 26.6 Reference systems & native component libraries

Don't invent a base system — adopt one and tokenize on top. The majors, and what each owns.

- **Material Design 3 / Material You (Google)** — the most complete open system: full token spec (ref/sys/comp), `Material Components` for Android + first-party Compose (`androidx.compose.material3`), dynamic color, adaptive layouts, Material Symbols. The Android default; also the de-facto base for Flutter. Trap: shipping stock Material to iOS — it reads as "an Android app on my iPhone."
- **Apple HIG + SwiftUI/UIKit (Apple)** — guidance-led rather than a downloadable token kit; the "components" are the OS frameworks themselves (UIKit/AppKit controls, SF Symbols, system materials). iOS 26 / macOS Tahoe 26 introduce **Liquid Glass** (WWDC 2025) as the cross-Apple material language — translucent, content-tinted, motion-reactive, reserved for the navigation layer over content (→ §1 materials). Trap: cloning Material's elevation/shadow vocabulary on Apple — Apple expresses depth through layering, vibrancy, and glass, not `dp` shadows.
- **Fluent 2 (Microsoft)** — the Windows / cross-platform system: full token pipeline, libraries for WinUI/WPF, web (`@fluentui/react-components`), iOS, Android, and macOS. **Acrylic** (background-blur material) and **Mica** (opaque, wallpaper-tinted window material, Windows 11) are its signature surfaces. Reach for it for Windows-first or Microsoft-ecosystem apps. Trap: Mica is for long-lived window backdrops, not transient/scrolling surfaces — misuse tanks perf and legibility.
- **Carbon (IBM)** — enterprise/data-dense system; React-first, also Angular/Vue/Web Components/Svelte, with a rigorous token + grid spec. Reach for it for B2B dashboards and admin tooling, not consumer mobile.
- **Polaris (Shopify)** — domain-specific system for building *on Shopify* (admin + embedded apps); strong content/voice guidance and React components, now under App Bridge. Reach for it only inside that ecosystem — it encodes Shopify's product, not a general base.
- **Build on top, don't fork** — adopt a reference system's tokens + components, then layer your brand at the semantic tier. Forking the component source to restyle is a maintenance death sentence — you lose every upstream a11y fix and OS-update adaptation. Trap: "we'll customize Material deeply" usually means you wanted a different system; pick the right base instead.

## 26.7 Governance, versioning & Figma↔code sync

A multi-platform system is only as consistent as its single source of truth and the discipline around changing it.

- **Single source of truth for tokens** — one canonical token store (often Figma variables or a Git-versioned `tokens.json`), generating *all* platform outputs via Style Dictionary. Two sources (designers edit Figma, engineers edit Swift constants) guarantee drift. Trap: letting platform code become a second source — outputs must be generated, never hand-edited.
- **Figma↔code sync** — Figma **Variables** (modes for light/dark/brand/density) map onto the semantic-token tier; export via Tokens Studio or the Variables REST API → DTCG JSON → Style Dictionary → platform code, ideally in CI. Modes are how one Figma file expresses every theme. Trap: Figma "styles" (the older primitive-ish construct) ≠ Variables — only Variables carry modes/aliasing needed for true semantic theming.
- **Semantic versioning of the system** — version the design system like a library (`MAJOR.MINOR.PATCH`). Token *removal* or *rename* is a breaking (major) change across N platforms simultaneously; deprecate-then-remove, never hard-delete. Trap: renaming a semantic token without a deprecation alias silently breaks every downstream platform at once.
- **Contribution & review model** — who can mint a primitive vs an alias vs a component token must be governed (central team owns primitives; product teams propose semantics). Ungoverned token sprawl is the most common way a "system" decays into a junk drawer. Trap: per-team one-off tokens with no review → thousands of near-duplicate colors, the exact problem tokens were meant to kill.
- **Per-platform release cadence** — platforms ship on different clocks (App Store review vs Play staged rollout vs web continuous vs a watchOS companion bound to the phone build). A token change can't go live everywhere at the same instant — design for version skew (additive changes, feature-flagged adoption). Trap: assuming atomic rollout — a semantic change that *requires* all platforms updated together strands whichever ships slowest.

## 26.8 The web-thinking trap (anti-patterns)

The thread running through this section: app design systems fail when web reflexes are applied unexamined to native targets.

- **Pixel breakpoints instead of size/window classes** — `@media (min-width: 768px)` thinking ported to apps. Native uses *size classes* (SwiftUI compact/regular horizontal+vertical; Material compact/medium/expanded window size classes), which abstract over device, orientation, multitasking split-view, and foldables. Trap: hardcoded breakpoints break the moment an iPad enters Slide Over or a foldable unfolds.
- **One control style for all platforms** — a custom-drawn toggle/picker shipped identically everywhere. Native users expect the *system* switch, picker wheel, and date control — muscle memory and a11y are tuned to them. Trap: a beautiful custom picker that no screen reader announces correctly — VoiceOver/TalkBack get nothing (→ §19).
- **CSS shadow/elevation vocabulary on Apple** — `box-shadow`-style elevation tokens don't translate; Apple wants vibrancy, layering, and glass. Trap: a token system whose only depth primitive is a numeric shadow can't express native iOS depth at all.
- **DOM/flexbox layout mental model** — native layout is constraints (Auto Layout), declarative stacks (`HStack`/`VStack`, `Row`/`Column` with weights), or Flutter's box/flex model — not the CSS box model. Porting flexbox assumptions produces fragile native layouts. Trap: nesting deep stacks to simulate `flex-grow` instead of using native spacers/weights.
- **Ignoring system-owned surfaces** — status bar, home indicator, Dynamic Island, navigation gesture zones, safe-area insets, foldable hinge, watch crown. Web has no analog; apps that draw into these without respect get clipped or hijack OS gestures. Trap: full-bleed backgrounds that ignore safe-area insets and put content under the home indicator (→ §19).
- **Treating the watch/wearable as a tiny phone** — watchOS / Wear OS demand glanceable, sub-5-second, complication-and-Tile-first interaction models, not shrunken phone screens. Tokens still apply (the type scale and color roles carry), but the component and IA layer is its own design. Trap: porting a phone navigation stack to a watch — wrong interaction altogether. (→ §24 wearables)
- **Assuming web reach == app reach on visionOS/spatial** — porting a flat 2D layout into a spatial container ignores depth, ornaments, and gaze+pinch targeting (visionOS minimum 60pt hit targets). Tokens for color/type survive; layout and input do not. Date-sensitive (2026): spatial is low-volume — tokenize for it only if it's a real target (→ §25 spatial).

*Sources: [Material Design 3 — Design tokens](https://m3.material.io/foundations/design-tokens/overview); [Apple Human Interface Guidelines](https://developer.apple.com/design/human-interface-guidelines); [Apple — Build a SwiftUI app with the new design (Liquid Glass, WWDC 2025)](https://developer.apple.com/videos/play/wwdc2025/323/); [Microsoft Fluent 2 design system](https://fluent2.microsoft.design/); [Style Dictionary — DTCG support](https://styledictionary.com/info/tokens/); [W3C Design Tokens Format Module (2025.10 draft)](https://www.designtokens.org/tr/drafts/format/).*
